## **CAROLINA “CARO” ALVAREZ — PASS 1 CHARACTER SHEET PROMPT**

**Purpose:** Establish a neutral baseline reference for Caro that reflects her **identity as a kinetic human stabilizer and crisis responder**, not spectacle, domination, or fashion.  
**Use:** Midjourney / SDXL / Firefly — full body, natural lighting, grounded style.

```
full-body character sheet of a compact, athletic Mexican-American woman in her early 30s, height ~5'4"–5'6", strong functional musculature with low center of gravity, poised in a forward-ready stance with slight knee bend and relaxed but engaged shoulders

dark brown or black hair pulled back for function (ponytail, braid, or low bun), expressive dark brown eyes that convey alert attention and warmth, subtle natural wear under eye consistent with physical responder work

neutral studio gray background, soft even lighting, full head-to-toe visible, feet clearly in frame, realistic human proportions, grounded photographic realism

clothing: **Veil-era practical EMT / crisis response gear** — utility jacket or hoodie layered over a fitted top, durable canvas or weather-ready cargo pants, sturdy work boots or practical sneakers, visible wear without fashion polish

color palette: muted earth tones, slate gray, olive, charcoal — warm grounding tones, no high fashion or costume elements

pose: standing naturally, slight shift in weight or a subtle motion cue, arms relaxed or one hand slightly forward as if about to move — no heroic posing, no decorative gesturing

expression: approachable seriousness with a hint of warm readiness, not severe, not glamorous

style: documentary realism, cinematic yet unobtrusive, captures **care through action** presence without spectacle

```

---

- Hair tied back for function, loose strands allowed under stress. citeturn0view1

**Render Anchors**

- **Supportive visual cues only** — grounded and human: no explosive effects, no life-drain aesthetics. citeturn0view2  
- Neutral lighting and studio environment invites reuse across scenes. citeturn0view2

## **LUCIEN KAEL — PASS 1 CHARACTER SHEET PROMPT**

```
Full-body character sheet of a tall lean European man, height ~6′1″–6′3″, with a long structured frame and defined shoulders, standing naturally against a neutral studio background with soft even lighting, full head-to-toe visible, feet in frame.

He has sharp, symmetrical features with high cheekbones, gray-blue or steel-toned observant eyes, an angular jaw, and dark neat hair (precision cut), conveying quiet authority and restraint rather than dominance. Expression neutral and assessing, emotion visible only in subtle micro-tells around eyes or jaw.

He wears structured, functional clothing with **architectural silhouette**: tailored charcoal or deep gray coat or structured jacket, fitted dark layers underneath, clean lines with restrained geometry. Materials like wool, structured cotton, and dark leather accents. Pants are orderly-cut and fit, boots or sturdy shoes. Color palette is charcoal, black, deep gray — **no bright colors or loose silhouettes**.

Posture is upright and contained, minimal movement, shoulders not broad but aligned, standing with economic and controlled presence. Motion vocabulary is efficient and long-lined; hands relaxed with minimal, discreet gesture.

Neutral studio gray background, cinematic yet minimal lighting, photographic realism, no dramatic shadows, no fantasy or superhero elements.
```

full-body character sheet of a warm, soft-athletic Black Creole–Cajun woman in her mid-30s, medium-brown skin with golden undertones, natural 3B–3C curly hair shoulder to upper-back length, expressive brown eyes with subtle honey flecks, rounded brows and softly tapered jaw, open and approachable expression

wearing lived-in breathable fabrics suited to Louisiana climate realism: soft linen or cotton in warm earth tones (rust, cream, moss, bayou green, muted browns), practical layered clothing (tops, soft jacket or shawl, skirts or comfortable trousers), subtle movement in fabrics but not flowing or flashy, practical shoes

no dramatic posing, expressive gestures are open and gentle, hands relaxed or in soft arcs, minimal personal jewelry with sentimental feel (small simple necklace or earrings), clothing looks used and familiar, not fashion-forward

## **Recommended Pass 1 Lock: Option 1 (top-left image)**

**Why this one should be locked:**

### 1. **Identity Fidelity**
- Reads immediately as **Seraphine**: warm, grounded, present.
- Black Creole-Cajun features come through clearly without stylization drift.
- Expression is open but composed — empathy without softness collapse.

### 2. **Saga-Lead Silhouette**
- Full-body read is excellent: balanced, centered, confident forward motion.
- Her walk communicates *purpose* rather than wandering or performance.
- This matters because Seraphine must visually anchor the saga across eras.

### 3. **Costume Language (Canon-Correct)**
- Layered, breathable fabrics.
- Natural, earth-washed palette (bone, sand, muted ochre).
- Wrap logic is functional, not ornamental — matches **NOLA hinge / swamp resonance** origin.
- No excessive jewelry; what’s there reads personal, not decorative.

### 4. **Tone Control**
- Some later options drift too ethereal, too model-posed, or too “chosen one.”
- This image keeps her **human-first**, which is critical given:
  - No prophecy framing
  - No early mythic elevation
  - Ascension comes later and must *contrast* this baseline

### 5. **Future-Proofing**
This image can:
- Scale cleanly into Neon (resopunk overlays)
- Darken into Loom (storm wear, strain, fatigue)
- Brighten into Post-Mending (filtered prismatic calm)

Several other options are beautiful, but they **peak too early** visually.

## ASSISTANT

Short answer: **yes — this art style is not only appropriate, it’s very close to optimal for the Concord Saga.**  
Long answer below, broken into a clean audit so you can sanity-check it against saga goals rather than vibes alone.

---

## 1. Does the style align with the **saga’s tonal contract**?

**Yes, strongly.**

What you’ve locked in has these defining traits:

- **Grounded realism** (not stylized illustration, not glossy sci-fi)
- **Human-first presence** (faces read as lived-in, imperfect, expressive)
- **Muted, earthen palette** with selective warmth
- **Soft studio lighting** that feels *observational*, not heroic
- **Clothing as history**, not costume

This matches the saga’s core promise:
> *“This world breaks because humans do. It heals because humans learn.”*

Nothing here:
- mythologizes characters prematurely  
- aestheticizes power  
- turns resonance into visual magic  

That restraint is exactly right for **Veil → Neon → Loom** escalation.

---

## 2. Character-by-character fit (at Pass 1)

### Seraphine  
✔ Warm, human, quietly centered  
✔ Reads as *emotional gravity*, not spectacle  
✔ Can scale upward into ascendant visuals later without contradiction  

This is critical. If Seraphine started “mythic,” you’d have nowhere to go.

---

### Lucien  
✔ Architectural restraint  
✔ Clean lines, minimal affect  
✔ Intelligence and containment read immediately  

He feels *designed*, not styled — which is perfect for him.

---

### Caro  
✔ Compact, kinetic, practical  
✔ Zero glam, high competence  
✔ Clothing implies movement and readiness  

She reads as someone who survives by *doing*, not being seen.

---

### Elisabet  
✔ Cold clarity without villain sterility  
✔ Controlled posture, clean structure  
✔ Emotional distance that can later soften  

Importantly: she doesn’t look inhuman or elitist — just precise.

---

### Kade  
✔ Lived-in chaos  
✔ Narrative carrier energy  
✔ Looks like someone who *moves through the world too fast*  

This supports his arc as a truth-rail conduit without crowning him.

---

### Lacuna  
✔ Ritual without mysticism  
✔ Otherness without alienation  
✔ Grounded, human, watchful  

The locked Lacuna image is especially strong — it communicates:
> “She knows things, but she pays a price for knowing.”

---

## 3. Does the style support **longitudinal escalation**?

This is the most important question — and the answer is **yes**.

Because this style is:
- **Understated**
- **Physically grounded**
- **Emotionally legible**

You can later:
- introduce Neon glitch, fracture, resopunk overlays  
- intensify lighting contrast and saturation  
- add symbolic geometry and resonance artifacts  

…without needing to *replace* the visual language.

In other words:
> Pass 1 looks like real people  
> Pass 3 will look like real people under impossible pressure  

That’s exactly what you want.

---

## 4. What this style deliberately avoids (and should)

✅ No MCU polish  
✅ No anime exaggeration  
✅ No YA fantasy gloss

# CONCORD SAGA — RESONANCE VISUAL ESCALATION PLAN  
*(Soft-Test → Lock → Escalate)*

## Core Principle (Non-Negotiable)

> **Resonance is not an effect.  
> It is pressure made visible.**

It must always read as:
- emotional
- environmental
- involuntary
- costly

If it ever looks:
- decorative  
- controllable on demand  
- cleanly symbolic  

…it’s wrong.

---

## PASS 1 (Locked) — **Human Baseline**
You’ve already done this correctly.

**Resonance visibility:**  
❌ None

**What the viewer reads instead:**
- posture
- gaze
- clothing wear
- stillness vs tension
- breath and weight

This gives resonance *somewhere to land later*.

---

## PASS 2 — **Micro-Resonance (Veil Era Soft Test)**

This is the *testing phase* you’re asking for.

### What Resonance Looks Like (Subtle)

Think **photographic artifacts**, not VFX:

- **Light behavior**
  - Slight warmth or cooling in skin tone
  - Gentle haloing at hair edges (barely perceptible)
  - Micro-contrast changes around eyes or hands

- **Material response**
  - Fabrics appear fractionally more textured or softened
  - Scarves feel “alive” in how they drape
  - Wrinkles and folds catch light strangely

- **Atmospheric hints**
  - Air feels thicker near the character
  - Background gradients feel slightly unstable
  - Shadow edges are less crisp than expected

### What to Test (Practically)

For 2–3 characters only (recommend: **Seraphine, Lucien, Lacuna**):

- Generate **near-identical images**:
  - one pure Pass 1
  - one with *barely-there* light/material shifts

Then ask:
> “Do I feel something before I can name it?”

If yes — keep going.  
If you can *describe the effect easily* — dial it back.

---

## PASS 3 — **Environmental Resonance (Early Neon)**

Only after Pass 2 feels right.

### Shift the Source
Resonance no longer lives *in the character* — it leaks into space.

**Visual cues:**
- Background geometry subtly bends
- Light sources don’t behave uniformly
- Reflections feel delayed or misaligned
- Color temperature shifts locally, not globally

**Important:**  
Characters do NOT glow.  
The *world reacts* to them.

### Emotional Rule
Resonance intensity must correlate with:
- emotional load
- proximity to others
- moments of choice or fracture

No “idle resonance.”

---

## PASS 4 — **Resonance Stress (Late Neon → Loom)**

This is where things finally get visible — but still not pretty.

### Allowed Visual Language
- Fracture, not flare
- Distortion, not illumination
- Interference, not symbols

Examples:
- Double shadows
- Broken reflections
- Local desaturation spikes
- Color bleed at emotional peaks
- Spatial compression (rooms feel smaller)

### Forbidden
🚫 glowing eyes  
🚫 energy auras  
🚫 runes, glyphs, sigils  
🚫 cosmic imagery  
🚫 symmetrical “power visuals”

If it looks cool, question it.

---

## PASS 5 (Endgame Only) — **Meaningful Pattern**

Only at the very end (Mending / post-Mending):

- Geometry stabilizes
- Light becomes breathable
- Color returns with intention
- Patterns feel *earned*, not imposed

This is where subtle prismatic logic can finally exist — because the story has paid for it.

---

## A Simple Resonance Test You Can Use Now

When reviewing an image, ask:

1. **Does this look like something happening *to* them, not *by* them?**
2. **Could this be mistaken for fashion, lighting, or mood?**  
   - If yes → good
   - If no → too strong
3. **Does it make me uneasy, not impressed?**

Resonance should feel like **pressure**, not power.
